# Kindred MC AI-player feature tracker
Version 2.0 · 15 October 2026

This is the only completion checklist. [The player contract](../minecraft_player_spec.md) defines the product; [architecture](player-architecture.md) defines boundaries. The companion roadmap has been replaced, not preserved as an alternate plan.

## Product and implementation order
Build autonomous AI players with personal needs/goals and real teamwork. Three must earn an empty-inventory survival playthrough through the Dragon. Follow/help are tasks; creator access is administration. Human presence and a mode toggle are not the new autonomy model.

First qualify the native player body (F-25b), then integrate local survival/recipe progression (F-25c with F-04/05/10..17 and E-04/07/12). Bring shared assignments E-11 into the core path, then exploration/Nether/equipment/End (E-14..17/39/40). Optional inference F-19 and social refinements must not displace these physical dependencies. No Jev or mandatory hosted orchestration.

Compatibility names, save fields and old command aliases are not instructions to rebuild the old concept. Historical checked foundations establish only their dated bounded evidence; none proves current player-body mechanics or a full release.

## Game modes (direction recorded 2026-10-20)
The owner set four core modes. Each is a parent feature below (M-01..M-04) on a shared mode and match framework (M-00) and schematic library (M-05). The player contract (MODE-01..05) and AGENTS.md describe them; the earned survival playthrough (E-40) stays the production objective and the foundation the survival modes are built on.
- **Co-op Survival (M-01):** progress at the human's natural pace; never seek out a boss fight, but be ready when the human is (revised 2026-10-23: boss fights are an optional progression, below); bases, farms, enchanting, and builds planned from schematics with every block earned.
- **Manhunt (M-02):** runner or hunter, between humans and AI players, including two or more AIs; the hunter gets iron-level gear and then hunts, going further only when it is opportune; the runner speedruns while evading.
- **Speedrun (M-03):** random seed; any% glitchless with the techniques the speedrunning community accepts.
- **Builder (M-04):** a creative build 1v1 on a random theme in a generated arena, with the presentation of a minigame server.

Direction recorded 2026-10-23: the owner introduces no further specs. Work sharpens what exists and moves toward the four core modes, the north star, plus one optional mode.
- **Co-op Survival (M-01)** is a regular playthrough with natural progression: a house or base, farms, a ranch with animal pens, horses tamed and ridden, XP farms, enchanting and the like. Boss fights are an optional progression (MODE-02).
- **Notes recorded later on 2026-10-23 (owner):**
  - **Needs are a queue.** Items a build or craft needs do not halt everything for one fetch after another. The player finishes what it can with what it holds and fills in what it has, then fetches the shortfall together in one trip planned for its time. An item urgent to finishing the task, or very easy to get (stored in a chest it knows), it fetches at once. Example: an enchanting room with too few bookshelves is built first, the table encased as far as the shelves go, then it goes for more.
  - **Manhunt (M-02).** Everyone starts from scratch. The runners' goal is to beat the game quickly but reliably, not at speedrun pace, while evading the hunters. The hunters' goal is to kill the runners while gearing up as the game goes on.
  - **Builds.** Huts serve for tests. The north star is detailed houses, bunkers and builds: several floors and, above all, functional.
  - **Staged progression (M-01).** A base starts small, to get the world started and give the player a place to store resources, and keeps improving: three stages, from a small base to bigger and better. The same staging holds for farms and other functional builds. Mob-spawner farms are rare, so each is a one-off functional build for grinding and resources.
- **Factions (M-06, optional)** is the same natural survival progression, but each AI player plays as an independent, unfriendly to the other players and AI players in it. It calls for more strategy: hidden bunkers, concealed resource farms, defences and scouting. D7 holds: only players who joined it are fair game.
- **Notes recorded later still on 2026-10-23 (owner):**
  - **Factions bases and bunkers in five stages (M-06a, M-06b).**
    - *The base.* A walled-in mini civilization that the player expands as its resource needs grow. Its farms, cattle, house and the rest are all enclosed in a walled, restricted, castle-like area. One of the later stages adds a moat and bridge.
    - *The bunker.* It follows the same five-stage, expanding logic: multi-level and multi-room, an underground mini civilization that grows as the game goes on.
    - *For both:* walls are always lined with something, and entrances are discreet.
  - **Redstone (M-01d).** Farms and other builds use working redstone mechanics.
  - **Repair (E-31a).** A player keeps what it built whole. A broken chest is replaced; what is blown up or destroyed is fixed. There is never a graveyard build left behind.
  - **One weighted list for everything it does (F-25c9).**
    - *Life first.* When hunger is low it gets food, enough to last. At night it sleeps.
    - *Builds near the bottom.* Ongoing builds come near the bottom of the list.
    - *A bed at home.* Home-like builds have beds, so the player's spawn is set there.
  - **Staying alive while evading (M-02c).**
    - *Keeping itself going.* While evading another player or a threat, a player queues what keeps it alive and gets it to safety as its hunger runs down. It eats at a safe distance to recover health, or changes course.
    - *Not leading them home.* In a world where others are unfriendly, it goes somewhere else rather than back to its base, which would give the base away.
  - **Builds grown from a seed block (E-29a).** The owner's idea, still being worked on: a player given a seed block (cherry wood, say) builds whatever it builds round that block. Builds come in different shapes and sizes, never the same static build repeated across worlds.

Recommended order: finish the survival core (F-25c4, then E-07 combat and caves, E-15 portal travel); then build M-00; M-04 can proceed in parallel because creative play does not depend on survival. After that come M-03, M-02 (built on the M-03 runner plus PvP), and the remainder of M-01 (schematic builds, enchanting, farms).

Decisions confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-20 (next focus: the survival core):
- **D6:** A mode rule may hand out an item only when it is announced and identical for human and AI participants, such as Manhunt's tracking compass for every hunter. Survival modes have no other exception to earned progress.
- **D7:** An AI player attacks a human only inside a match that human joined; outside matches, player attacks stay refused.
- **D8:** Kindred ships a small library of bundled schematics whose licenses allow it, and users may supply their own: files they provide, or single links they give, used within each site's terms and the creator's license, with attribution kept. No bulk scraping.

## Work protocol
Rows and checklist items go at their ID's place, never appended at the end: each table and each level of the checklist is kept in ID order (numbers compared as numbers, so F-22c4 before F-22c40), and `support.py tracker` fails otherwise. PRs adding rows then touch different lines (2026-11-10).
Claim an open ID before implementation. Preserve IDs; add explicit children. Put a LIVE EDIT marker first while editing contracts; remove it when finished. Check all acceptance criteria before marking done, with date/evidence and affected user docs in the same change. Reopen invalid evidence; keep blocked work open. Native outcomes require native evidence; live scenarios require real clients/runs. Use [the evidence template](evidence/README.md). Update the manifest only with supported capabilities/qualified gates.

| F-19f | Done 2026-11-15 | Claude, Session B (claude/f19f-cache-route-1115) | companion-model: `ClaudeStrategist`, `OpenAiCompatibleStrategist`, `StrategistProvider`, `Strategists`; the release jar (`release-manifest.json`, D3) | Every stance consultation reported `0 cached`. Cause, from RUNNER 2's keyed run on main `399aeb7d` (15 calls, every one `cache read 0, cache write n/a`): the run took the OpenAI-compatible route to `api.anthropic.com/v1`, as a base URL selects, and Anthropic's compatibility layer does not support prompt caching (its `prompt_tokens_details` "Always empty"). The native route (`ClaudeStrategist`, the Anthropic Java SDK) caches, its prompt already in order (the stable system text, instructions and guide, with `cache_control`; the fixed schema; then the growing report). Measured by RUNNER 2 on a development build from main `2657188`: the first call wrote 6,183 tokens and each of the 14 after it read 6,183, over about 8 minutes; cached calls 1,160 to 1,985 ms against 3,785 ms. The SDK is on the development classpath only: bundling it in the release jar is the owner's decision; meanwhile keyed runs that need the cache use a development build (the lead, 2026-11-15). [evidence](evidence/F-19f.md) |

Corpus gate cleared by the marker rule: 0 ranch lines in six full runs. The PR's CI (#431, run 44955711811) green; merged as `cb40c52`. [evidence](evidence/F-22c105.md) |
| F-22c106 | Done 2026-11-21 | REFACTOR (claude/f22c106-paid-margin-1121), the lead's assignment and the lead's approval of the narrower fix | adapter: `PlayerRuntime` (`endsItsPeriodWithin`, added to the skill tick's `usable` line; test seam `holdSkillTicksForTest`, off by default); gametest: `NativeAccountingGameTests` (`aWalkWhoseTicksRunLongAtItsPeriodsEndCarriesOnIntoTheNext`, new; `walkOverPeriodEnd`, shared) | `aWalkRunningAtItsPeriodsEndOnASlowStoreCarriesOnIntoTheNext` failed on #408 (run 43868643269) and #417 (run 44905765686): "native_goto BLOCKED: Movement authority denied". A tick begun on a slice's last milliseconds outlasted it; `paid()` reads the clock at each call, so the walk's own move was refused inside the tick, and the period's hold never saw it. Fixture: the seam holds such a tick for `RENEW_GAIN_MIN`; without the fix the new test failed 3 of 3 with CI's message. Fix: a walk or wait whose slice is booked to its period's end is not begun with `RENEW_GAIN_MIN` or less left, and is held over instead. The one new behaviour: up to 250 ms of the last slice goes unused at a period's end. A lapse at the hours' end, the cap or a late mid-period renewal is unchanged. A wider margin, on every busy skill, failed `aRunningSkillStopsWhenItsHoursEnd` 3 of 3 (halted 218 ms early, the hours still open) and was not kept. The class ×3: 24 of 24. Corpus gate: passes, 3 flagged and all cleared; no scene crossed a period's end. [evidence](evidence/F-22c106.md) |
| F-22c107 | Done 2026-11-22 | REFACTOR (claude/f22c-bunker-giveup-diag-1121, then claude/f22c107-break-tie-1122), the lead's assignment | adapter: `BuildRunner` (`logCannotPlaceYet`), diagnostic, log only; core: `ConstructionPlanner` (`cellsOnLine`, `TIE`); test: `ConstructionPlannerTest` | `NativeBunkerGameTests.itDigsOutLinesAndFurnishesItsHideoutUnderTheGround` placed 343 of 345 blocks on CI (#397 run 43829145998, #417 run 44883669995): "The rest are blocks it cannot place yet", the two left the shed roof's last planks at y −46. Alone, it passes 3 of 3. When the planner gives up that way, the diagnostic logs each block left, with what the site reads in its cell, what the world holds there, its rule, and whether it was given up on, refused or came out wrong, once for each distinct set. Read on #457's CI (run 45025077528): one block left, dirt in the way at y −52, its break refused three times as out of sight from a spot whose eyes' line crossed an x and a z face at exactly the same point; the planner stepped z first through cleared cells, the server x first into dirt. The fix takes both cells on a tie (`cellsOnLine`, `TIE` 1e-6), so a break's look is clear only where the server's is; untied lines and placements unchanged. Its three pure tests: the first two failing on main's planner, all 107 construction tests passing after. The nine build classes ×3 on each side: 99 of 99 passing on both, scaffold columns 512 against 513, nothing given up. Only builds reach it (`BuildRunner`, from `PlayerRuntime` and `RanchRunner`), and the corpus has no build lines, so no corpus gate. None for F-22c107: a block given up can still be walled in before "Once more" tries it, which the tie no longer leads to. [evidence](evidence/F-22c107.md) |

| F-22c110 | Done 2026-11-22 | Claude (CI GREEN; claude/ci-green-1122-searim), a CI census child at the lead's word | test only: `NativeFactionOpenWaterGameTests` (`island()`'s rim; `itsSeaKeepsItsWaterWithinTheGroundItKeeps`, new) | `NativeBuildBattleGameTests.twoAiBuildersBuildTheThemeInCreativeAndVoteOnEachOther` failed on CI twice (43782657628, one vote; 44918551355, "Nobody voted"): a voter took the deep-water escape on its walkway and its walk was blocked. The open-water sea laid in the batch before it had no rim, so its water ran out to ±(SEA+1) and fell below the box it keeps; the restore, with no neighbour updates, left the curtain. It crossed the BuildBattle field in exactly the two failures (sea edge z 8520173 and 4378685 inside the field; 11211069 and 6005421 outside it in two passes). Fix: a stone rim at ±(SEA+1), floor to top, inside its kept box. Fixture (STAGED): water outside its kept ground 200 ticks on, 0 of 3 before (about 2,000 cells), 3 of 3 after; the class ×3 5 of 5 each. On #442's CI the check counted a replay scene's own water two blocks past the box, and with no player near the sea's chunks often ran no fluid: it now counts only water not there before the island was laid (a staged source in a cup must still stand) and simulates the island's chunks; without the rim 0 of 3 (3,560 to 5,840 cells), with it 6 of 6. The early-choice intermittent (#427) is not this leak: no open-water sea near it. Other fixtures that may leak are F-22c111's. #442's CI (run 44998600010, head `85f9f855`) green, the BuildBattle test among the passes; merged as `ae9c4401`. [evidence](evidence/F-22c110.md) |
| F-22c111 | In progress 2026-11-22 | Claude (CI GREEN; claude/ci-green-1122-fluidshell), filed by F-22c110 at the lead's word | gametest: `FixtureGround` (`restore`'s shell); `NativeFixtureGroundGameTests` (new) | `FixtureGround.restore` puts a kept box back without updating its neighbours, so fluid a fixture let past its box stays in the world for later tests: it broke BuildBattle's vote (F-22c110) and, on #441 (44973451122/1), the bring case 7 boat pit, flooded by the open-water causeway sea's spill (F-22c103b closed as F-22c110's case). Full suite on `5238e6bc` ×3 each side: 940/940 each with it, 939/939 on main. Cost: 163 ms of shell work over the suite against 5,117 ms of block put-back. Cleared in the three runs: the four open-water seas (about 10.4k to 11.3k cells each, without F-22c110's rim), the Boat sea (2,842 to 3,664), WaterBox (1,072), Spire (66), and a few cells in Gold, Nether, PortalCampaign and hub tests. | F-22c112 | Done 2026-11-22 | Claude, Session B (claude/doorway-plank-1122), at the lead's word | adapter: `BuildRunner` (`finished`: a block's drop not spent by a walk that completed out of reach with the drop still lying, up to `SHORT_ENDS` 3 times, with a marker line; `pickUp` records `woolBy`, `woolTimes`); gametest: `NativeBuildNeedsGameTests.aWalkThatEndsShortOfADropDoesNotSpendIt` (new, staged); docs: `evidence/F-22c112.md`, `evidence/eval/f22c112/` | `NativeCompoundGameTests.withADoorwayPlankLeftLyingItPicksItUpToShutTheDoorway`'s "1 blocks not in their state … air for oak_planks" (main 44969254087, #386's 43800148349, F-22c100, F-22c93). | F-31j | In progress 2026-11-16 | REFACTOR (claude/f31-body-maps-1116), the lead's assignment | companion-server-adapter: `PlayerRuntime` (`logCost`, `bodyMaps`, `BODY_MAPS`; read only); gametest: `NativeCostGameTests`; no body behaviour | The body's position-keyed maps (`placeRefusals`, `placeSetAside`, `chestsUsed`, `seenAtStores`, `untrustedStores`, `recentlyTaken`, and `raids`) sized for the month run: each one's key, what adds to it and what removes or ages it, with file:line. Bounded in the code: place refusals (≤ 65), chests used (≤ 32, never aged), seen at stores and recently taken (aged 5 minutes). Never shrinking: untrusted stores; raid decisions, never expired; set-asides pruned only on the workstation step. At 30 days and 8 players the worst cases are some thousands of small entries, a few MB, so none is a memory risk. Their staleness is the issue. **`chestsUsed` is a month-run functional limit, not a memory one.** It notes every chest it sees anyone open in a pvp match, teammates' and earlier matches' included. It prunes only opponents' chests, never ages, and empties only at a restart. Once full at 32, it refuses every new chest, so it decides and makes no new raid (E-44's `raidCandidates`/`askRaid`), silently, until a restart. Only pvp matches reach it (duel, manhunt; not the faction world, which is not a match, and no corpus file), so it would show in Manhunt played often on one server, where a side's own chests fill it from match to match. On the cost line it reads as that player's third figure at 32 while its seventh (`raids`) goes flat. Each player's sizes are now on the daily cost line. Remaining: the month run's line to read them off; four bounds proposed for the owner and the lead after the freeze, `chestsUsed` first, not made ([evidence](evidence/F-31j.md)); `tools/soak/preflight.py` parses the field (`bodyMapsByPlayer`, its growth `bodyMapsPerGameDay`), and WATCHes a player whose chests used reach the code's cap of 32. At p99 one step is a whole 50 ms tick, for one player. How often it runs: `BuildRunner.next` calls `ConstructionPlanner.next` once per call unless a placement it walked to is due (`placeWalkedTo`); it is called from `buildCandidates`, which runs when a player's routine candidates are refreshed: by the attention scheduler, every 5 ticks for routine goals and every 20 for equipment readiness while routine, every 10 and 5 in recovery, not at all while alert or in combat (`AttentionSchedulePolicy.cadenceTicks`). So a player building runs it up to about every 5 ticks; eight building at once, about 8 × 25 ms over 5 ticks, 40 ms a tick on average, before anything else. What the eight-player faction run already shows with bases on (factions8b-4ba244e, M-06i's evidence): mean tick 19.25, 26.21 and 26.25 ms; worst 521, 774 and 622 ms; ticks over 50 ms 271, 757 and 692 in 72,000; the hitches' cause not attributed. ## Canonical checklist
Completed entries below retain their original bounded evidence. Reopened or unfinished entries stay unchecked. Historical IDs remain stable; obsolete mode UI is retired while save compatibility remains.

- [ ] F-01 — Live client, identity, access and movement-stop qualification.
- [ ] F-02 — Courtesy and shared attention during movement, speech and work.
- [ ] F-03 — Home, bounded area, assigned storage and permission setup.
- [x] F-03a — Bounded native resource-work patches. Completed 2026-10-15; [scope/bounds evidence](evidence/F-03a.md).
- [ ] F-04 — Registered skill runner, checkpoints, soft pause and deterministic controls.
- [x] F-04a — Bounded prepaid native wait and eating skills. Completed 2026-10-15; [native paid-skill evidence](evidence/F-04a.md).
- [x] F-04b — Mid-skill danger interruption and cancellation settlement barrier. Completed 2026-10-17; [evidence](evidence/F-04b.md).

Completed 2026-11-01: `KINDRED_EVAL` and `eval-result.json`, `tools/eval` (`run.py`, `score.py`, `compare.py`), medians and ranges over ten seeds, earned or staged said; the two-game-hour run, the night fix against the build before it (no clear difference), the E-49 build's arms, and the final build's ten seeds, all complete and earned. Summaries only under `docs/evidence/eval/`. [Evidence](evidence/F-26.md).
  - [x] F-26a — Work handed back to the server thread runs within a tick on a server sprinting through its ticks, as the evaluation runs it, or one too loaded to keep up: vanilla held each such task until it was more than three ticks old, every task waited on two to four of them, and a step of a branch mine took about 100 ticks in the evaluation against 42 off a sprint (45 of 144 stuck spells on `f25c4ax-84b331c` were branch mines for iron). Completed 2026-11-04: the sprinting branch-mine fixture fails 3 of 3 on `f0258b6` (a stuck spell after 55 to 73 blocks, about 104 ticks a block) and passes 3 of 3 with the fix (160 blocks in 6,772 to 6,802 ticks); `bash ./gradlew build` 929 JUnit tests, none failing. [Evidence](evidence/F-26a.md).
- [x] F-27 — Death replay: a native player's death captured as a structure and its state, promoted to a GameTest that must reproduce it before a fix and pass after. Proposed 2026-10-31; [design](proposals/next-levers.md). Completed 2026-10-31: a drowning from an evaluation run (`echo-50149`, seed 101) died again 3 of 3 before its fix and lived 3 of 3 after it in each of seven runs of the class alone; the capture 3.4 to 6.3 ms of the server thread's time in its fixture (25 runs); the generator found by the suite with no registration; the conventions in validation; a first replay (`sol-60203`, a brawl) not committed, as it dies about half the time with its fix or without, the fix its log showed kept with a fixture of its own (0 of 3 before, 3 of 3 after); [evidence](evidence/F-27.md).

Proposed 2026-11-10. In progress 2026-11-10 (`claude/corpus-1110`): the suite, `tools/corpus` (`import.py`, `report.py`, `gate.sh`), 224 death scenes and one staged fixture, the baseline on a7b6b9b ([evidence](evidence/F-27a.md)). Remaining: stuck-spell scenes (none yet), Factions-mode scenes beyond factions-110-d5057a4's 22 (added 2026-11-10, played as survival: a replay keeps no mode state), the fixture scenes of F-35a/b/c, and the gate made the merge rule.

- [x] F-28 — A headless simulator for the brain: the core's planners and policies run on a fake world in seconds, scored like a real run. Proposed 2026-10-31; [design](proposals/next-levers.md). Completed 2026-10-31: `companion-sim`, a module on `companion-core` alone, plays three packs of a hundred seeds (dusk on open ground, a cave, empty-handed toward the portal) in 35.1 s through the core's own decision loop, policies and planner, its log lines scored by `RunMetrics` unchanged; it tests decisions and plans, not execution. Its check for iron left in a furnace found the planner walking off from its smelting iron (55 of 100 caving runs, 214 iron in all; none after the fix), confirmed by `NativeFurnaceWaitGameTests`, 0 of 1 before the fix and 3 of 3 alone in each of two runs after, and by a planner test that fails before it and passes after. Its deaths, 0.18 a game hour, against the server's 0.60 in the first hour on the same core, over the ten seeds: a factor of 3.3 (2.5 for the four mobs it has; first filed as 0.48 and 2.7, from runs with seed 404's missing starters counted, then as 0.59 and 2.6 over the nine other seeds, F-26), the gap recorded with what it does not model (water, falls, the biome's own mobs, 1.21's caves, a team). Design changes: one player a run, `ExpeditionPlanner` not driven, spiders added; [evidence](evidence/F-28.md).
- [x] F-29 — Rescue plans: when stuck, a bounded plan of one to six installed skills from the model, validated by the proposal gate and revalidated at dispatch. Proposed 2026-10-31; [design](proposals/next-levers.md). Completed 2026-11-01: `rescue.RescuePlan`, the bridge's rescue lane, the plan carried out a step at a time as ordinary tasks; eight GameTests 3 of 3 (the pit escaped; seven steps, an unknown step, an uninstalled skill and a stale fence refused with nothing moved). The first arm found plans refused as stale (the fence now leaves out the player's own task starts) and steps misread (the instructions now say what each does). On the final build's ten seeds, rescue on against off: stuck game minutes 39.0 [3 to 63] against 42.0 [12 to 99], no clear difference; 34 asked, none stale, 4 of 31 plans carried out got the player out. [Evidence](evidence/F-29.md).

Completed 2026-11-09: on `cd56501`, rescue off against on, the same seeds. At 2 h on 20 seeds, stuck minutes 18 [0 to 63] against 4.5 [0 to 33], 71 of 145 spells out. At 4 h on 10 seeds, 49.5 [3 to 93] (and a second off sample, 63 [30 to 165]) against 21 [6 to 228], 47 of 111 spells out. Only the rescue's own measures differ clearly. A lit portal on 10 of 20 against 15 of 20, and 5 of 10 against 8 of 10, the ranges overlapping. 54,000 to 87,000 tokens a seed. [evidence](evidence/F-29e.md).
- [x] F-30 — The atlas: explored chunks, frontiers, sightings, priors and a danger map, used by expeditions, mining and routes. Proposed 2026-10-31; [design](proposals/next-levers.md). Completed 2026-11-01 against its acceptance: `atlas.Atlas` and `atlas.Priors` (the priors checked against 1.21.1's generation), kept per player in `<uuid>.atlas.json`, fed by the goal's sweep and by each hit and death; its users built: ore it saw gone back to when it is wanted, a walk's cost at night through the cells where it was hurt or killed, and a report line. 8 JUnit; `NativeAtlasGameTests` 3 of 3 in each of three runs alone; with the atlas off, the diamonds and the night walk 0 of 1 each, the day control passing either way. Ten seeds with the atlas against without, both on the E-49 build: no clear difference, the first iron ingot at 10.75 [4.7 to 31.2] game minutes against 11.75 [5.8 to 27.3], diamonds on 3 and 2 of the ten seeds; 112 leads back to iron it had seen. Not built from the design: exploring toward the frontier, the fortress and stronghold searches, the team's share-out, bases as shelters; [evidence](evidence/F-30.md).

- [x] E-47 — Learning from the server's people: with each person's consent, the server's customs (mining depth, shelter time, palette, where people are) as bounded biases; nothing from chat or inventories. Proposed 2026-10-31; [design](proposals/next-levers.md). Completed 2026-11-01: `/aiplayer watch on|off`, said by each person for themselves (the console, a command block, an AI player and `/execute as` refused); only the height of the coal, iron and diamond ore a consenting person breaks is learned, as counts in four-block bands, each person's apart so that `off` deletes exactly theirs, in the world's `kindred/customs.json` too; nothing from chat or inventories (D10). Twenty iron ore mined at y 12 by a consenting mock person moved an AI player's iron depth from 16 to 15 (three tenths of the way, never more than eight blocks); twenty at y −40 by one who had not opted in changed nothing, nor did her diamonds or her chat; `watch off` put it back to 16 and the file then held neither of them. JUnit 5 of 5; the GameTest 3 of 3 alone, and 0 of 1 with the campaign not handed the customs. Design changes: only the mining depth is built (shelter time, palette, hours, heat map and settling are not, and nothing is gathered for them); a person's counts kept apart so that deletion is exact; the prior blended is the campaign's own depth, as the atlas's priors do not set it; [evidence](evidence/E-47.md).

- [ ] E-65 — Automation: the crafter for the recipes it repeats, item sorters, bubble elevators and piston doors, flying machines and a dripstone lava farm, blast mining under policy and never in Factions. Proposed 2026-11-10; [design](proposals/the-rest-of-the-game.md).
- [ ] E-66 — Potions, food and effects in play: golden apples at the threshold, milk and honey, stews, potions by situation as wants, 1.21's bottles and arrows, cold and air. Proposed 2026-11-10; [design](proposals/the-rest-of-the-game.md).
- [ ] E-67 — Villagers beyond trading: breeding and a bell, curing for discounts, golems and the iron farm, the wandering trader and llamas, hero and pariah. Proposed 2026-11-10; [design](proposals/the-rest-of-the-game.md).
- [ ] E-68 — Expression, the rest: signs in its words, paintings by taste, trophies and heads, an ender chest, a lodestone compass, a library of books, fireworks at a milestone. Proposed 2026-11-10; [design](proposals/the-rest-of-the-game.md).
- [ ] E-69 — Advancements as goals: every advancement mapped to needs and places, the hard ones as projects, a scoreboard, a brag in character. Proposed 2026-11-10; [design](proposals/the-rest-of-the-game.md).
- [ ] E-70 — Thrifty habits (owner, 2026-11-11: implemented regardless of the performance work). Proposed 2026-11-11; [design](proposals/thrifty-travel.md).
  - [ ] E-70a — Nights in bed: in by dusk as default, a bed carried and used in its sealed pocket, in bed when a person sleeps, never holding up a person's night, night work only on purpose. [design](proposals/thrifty-travel.md).
    - [x] E-70a1 — A bed is never propped in a floor cell the build set aside: the set-aside block stays owed, a block that rests only on it waits on it, and the cabin is finished. Completed 2026-11-12; [evidence](evidence/E-70a1.md).
    - [ ] E-70a2 — A bed is not kept from it by wool of mixed colours: it hunts or shears for the colour it holds most of; why most bedless runs never pick up wool is read first.
  - [ ] E-70b — The beaten path: trail memory in the atlas and a chunk price in every choice, from its own knowledge only, never the server's loaded state. [design](proposals/thrifty-travel.md).
  - [ ] E-70c — Travel like a veteran: a trip planner choosing among the ways it has (walk, boat, the Nether, rail, ice road, mount, elytra) by time, chunk price and risk. [design](proposals/thrifty-travel.md).
- [ ] E-71 — Veteran mechanics (owner, 2026-11-12). Proposed 2026-11-12; [design](proposals/veteran-mechanics.md).
  - [ ] E-71a — Timed-crouch bridging: backward, crouched only at the edge, a run of blocks as one skill with a record each; the held crouch over a deadly drop or under threat. [design](proposals/veteran-mechanics.md).
    - [x] E-71a1 — The `native_bridge` action and its acceptance list: the timed crouch, the held crouch over a deadly drop or under threat, a refused block left crouched at the edge, the route's bridge steps as one run. Completed 2026-11-12; [evidence](evidence/E-71a.md).
  - [ ] E-71b — Work from where it stands: every block in reach from one stance before it moves. [design](proposals/veteran-mechanics.md).
    - [ ] E-71b1 — Placing from where it stands: the farthest in reach from its feet first; then the spot serving the most of the layer. Open 2026-11-16, not landed; [evidence](evidence/E-71b1.md).

  - [ ] E-71d — Hands and feet together: eating on the move, the tool out on arrival, torches from the off hand. [design](proposals/veteran-mechanics.md).
  - [ ] E-71e — The rhythm against mobs: the sprinting hit and its reset, in and out of reach, creepers and skeletons by the shield, a doorway and high ground. [design](proposals/veteran-mechanics.md).

- [ ] F-35g — Of F-35, the movement series. Under the sea, the water over its head: its own ceiling opened, its bucket filled from the source it saw over it, resealed at once and its tunnel let drain; a climb under water too wide to get out from under handed back; no "to the wall" into the cell it went out from under the water from (606's Kindred in iteration 18, `e40a18`: 12 stuck spells, "out from under the water" and "to the wall" some thirty times each a spell). In progress 2026-11-11: the fixtures pass, 606 live to come; [evidence](evidence/F-35g.md).
- [ ] F-35h — Of F-35, the movement series. A faction walk out of a basin: refused for no way there, it makes a way, a step dug or a block of its own put, and gets there (Factions seed 109). Fixtures done 2026-11-11; the paired seeds remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35h.md).
- [ ] F-35h2 — Of F-35, the movement series. Dug in for the night with its faction play's place outside, an AI player asks no faction walk and makes no way out through its own seal till daylight, by the check its portal walk uses (Factions seed 110: Echo's made way dug its seal, the goal sealed it, 52 times). Fixture measured and the gate passed 2026-11-12; a live run remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35h2.md).
  - [x] F-35h2b — At nightfall, not yet dug in, a faction walk other than DEFEND yields to the goal's dig-in, never for nothing (only while the goal's step is a dig-in); the player shelters on its own land, not out on another's where it may dig nothing. Done (2026-11-14, #275 merged as `65332e15`): [evidence](evidence/F-35h2b.md).
- [ ] F-35i — Of F-35, the movement series. Faction walks back off as a whole: once two places have each had their three tries and been set aside with no walk between, none is asked for a while, longer each time, until it walks somewhere, moves 32 blocks or comes up (Factions seeds 109 and 303). Fixtures done 2026-11-11; the paired seeds remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35i.md).
  - [x] F-35i2 — Of F-35i: its at-surface test from what the player perceived (the sky's light at its feet), not the world's height map, for the refused walk's record, the pause and open water. Done (2026-11-22, #443 merged as `f8b7ffea`): [evidence](evidence/F-35i2.md).
    - [ ] F-35i2a — Why bases on loses claims with F-35i2. Open (2026-11-22): two releases of the wait read, both blocked bases off; [evidence](evidence/F-35i2a.md).
    - [ ] F-35i2b — A native fixture for F-35i2's `openWater` caller, staged in water. Open (2026-11-22), not for now.
- [ ] F-35j — Of F-35, the movement series. A faction walk into open sea: where a walk would end in open water the player saw (all round its end within sight, or out to the edge of its sight beyond), the place is set aside with no walk asked; water it has not seen is asked as before (Factions seed 303). Fixtures, the gate and the seeds with builds differing done 2026-11-11; the pair with F-35j alone switched run 2026-11-12, a defect on seed 110 (F-35j1 to fix); [evidence](evidence/F-35j.md).
- [ ] F-35j1 — Of F-35, the movement series. A refused faction walk serves its wait before it is looked at again as open water, a place set aside as open water counts toward F-35i's pause, and the next choice after it comes at the decision interval (Factions seed 110: a refusal storm, ten places in a second). Fixtures measured, the gate passed and seed 110 run on it 2026-11-12; a live run remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35j1.md).
- [ ] F-35k — Of F-35, the movement series. Off a ledge over a cavern: a player stranded two whole spells over on a ledge, every way off it a drop or water, begins its next move elsewhere at once, and where that walk and its tunnels are refused it makes a way, bridged, stepped or dug (e40a20's 8675309). Scenes measured 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12, the gate passed; a live run remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35k.md).
- [ ] F-35l — Of F-35, the movement series. Stone mined from a thin floor over a drop: a block below its feet is not chosen to mine where breaking it would leave no ground within a safe drop under it, so a floor one thick is not mined out from round where it stands (#224's back-off fixture: its sky island's floor, and a fall of thirty). Fixtures measured and the gate passed 2026-11-12; a live run remaining; [evidence](evidence/F-35l.md).
